## 2026-09-27 — Does the canonical history integral determine an event impulse?

The next “do 1” accepts examination of whether the unchanged canonical history integral determines a finite instantaneous velocity change at the certified wake-speed event. The first distinction is between the direct measure carried by positive-delay causal incidences on a fixed path and a weak limit obtained while changing histories or regularization. The direct event fiber has no admitted self incidence, because the incoming history is strictly subunit and the diagonal is excluded. The investigation tests whether any finite direct measure can nevertheless assign an atom at the event, and whether a bounded-variation velocity could satisfy the resulting measure equation. Independent review will check the positivity, support, finite-variation and population-remainder hypotheses. A scoped basename/path search with rg over scripts, tests and the master-equation-closure JSON, Python, JavaScript and shell sources found no byte binders for the five shared synthesis owners or the new event-measure family within that scope.

The completed [event-measure subject](analysis/smooth-two-particle-event-measure.md) and [independent assessment](analysis/smooth-two-particle-event-measure-independent-adjudication.md) accept a stronger negative result. A direct positive measure carried by strict positive-delay incidences gives zero event atom because its incoming self fiber is empty. The joined measure equation therefore gives equal velocity traces. For a bounded-variation velocity, these traces produce a common positive self cone; the measure inequality forces a unique simple pre-event emission root. Its graph is Lipschitz on compact outgoing intervals even when the receiver velocity has jumps or singular continuous variation. The canonical reciprocal-delay identity then forces infinite positive reception weight, contradicting local finiteness. No future absolute continuity or ordinary acceleration density is required.

The interpretation requirements are explicit: genuine positive incidence weighting, canonical agreement on every simple fixed-old-source graph including a locally Lipschitz receiver, finite scalar weight over compact reception windows and no diagonal boundary measure. The formal delta is not asserted to define a pullback on every nonsmooth path. The same uniform population-position neighborhood keeps the complete cross field a bounded continuous density. These are a conditional measure-class nonexistence theorem and its application, not universal impossibility of every distributional model.

The independently reviewed [domain audit](analysis/smooth-two-particle-event-measure-domain.md) distinguishes fixed-path cutoff removal from varying-history weak limits. Nested restrictions of one finite measure converge in total variation and retain the zero event atom. Divergent positive weight has no finite positive measure limit. Different families can concentrate into an atom, but regular-chart agreement alone does not fix the concentration or establish a coupled solution. An exact prescribed positive-range chart demonstrates that a reception atom is not universally banned by diagonal exclusion; the actual event's empty fiber is decisive. That chart and elementary concentrating-density controls are mathematical examples, not new lattice histories or physical solutions.

The subject's literal delta notation and off-root range/direction definitions were corrected before freezing. Review also made the Lipschitz-receiver chart requirement explicit so that agreement cannot become vacuous on irregular receiver paths. Manuscript Section 6.26, post-restart Section 12, current strategy and queue carry the reviewed result. Adjacent ordinary-equation limitations now point to the stronger measure result. Separate read-only synthesis checks confirm the scope, regularity, positivity and varying-history distinction. The completed direct-integral task is removed from the execution list. Reachability from a fully self-consistent preparation is proposed as a separate physical question, without changing this experiment's past or accepting a new task.

#### Starting-state clarification

There is no common initial state or common retained history across the three first-wave investigations. They share the canonical causal-root geometry, persistent labels and numerical normalization $c_f=1$. They are separate mathematical controls, not successive stages of one evolving population.

The finite contact study prescribes four complete pasts: targets stationary at $(\pm2,0,0)$ until $T=-1$, then the smooth cubic join to $(\pm0.01,0,0)$ with inward speed $2$ at release $T=0$; environmental labels remain at $(\pm1,1.5,0)$ through release. Charge magnitudes are one and coupling is $10^{-6}$. All four labels evolve under the EOM after release. Its earlier past is supplied, not proved to be an all-time EOM solution.

The population construction uses an infinite alternating-polarity cubic lattice with spacing $\ell=L/100$. Its stationary control stays at lattice anchors for the entire past; independent small smooth history bumps give additional nonstationary examples that return to the same release positions and zero velocities. This establishes an initial-history class, not an evolved dense population. Embedding the finite contact construction into this class remains unproved.

The provenance control prescribes two collinear opposite-polarity paths on $[-8,1]$: the first rests at $-4$ until $-2$, follows $x_1(t)=2t$ until zero, then rests at zero; the second is $x_2(t)=1/2-x_1(t)$. At reception start zero their separation is $1/2$ and both right-sided velocities are zero. The corner histories and the future reception segment are prescribed, not EOM-evolved. No complete all-earlier physical history is certified by this bounded instrument.

Within the fifteen provenance tests, tests 1–9 mutate structural certificate fixtures and do not supply physical starting trajectories. Test 10 uses stationary positions zero and two on $[-4,1]$. Tests 11–13 use the ten-branch prescribed pair above, with ledger mutations or equivalent subdivisions. Test 14 replaces one stationary path by $x_1(t)=t$ to exercise field-speed degeneracy, then supplies unsupported representation/format cases. Test 15 uses $x_1(t)=0$ and $x_2(t)=3+2t$ on $[-4,1]$, giving release positions zero and three and velocities zero and two. These starting inputs were checked directly against the focused test source and analytical owners; they are not inferred from the passing-test count.

A shared downstream benchmark would need one declared family of complete histories, finite restrictions, polarity/coupling, common release data, a selected and justified infinite summation rule, and compatible root margins. The current queue first reviews the proposed class, then develops summation/derivative/evolution and exhaustion. Common release positions and velocities alone would not define the same delayed system: different earlier paths can produce different received acceleration.

### Control-selection preference

The operator prefers minimal two-particle opposite-polarity EOM controls and fewer invented prescribed-path examples. Reuse the existing stationary/mirror and straight-line analyses before adding another geometry. Initial positions and velocities must be accompanied by declared retained histories; prescribe the input past only, and evolve the future when testing EOM reachability. A certified two-particle contact suffices for an existential claim on its admitted class; failure in that case does not prove universal exclusion, and coordinate contact does not by itself prove self-birth. Existing four-particle evidence is retained at its bounded scope without making further elaborate examples the default.

### Effort Contracts

1. **Independent finite-event self-boundary review.** Freeze analysis/mec-008-self-boundary-complement.md. Independently adjudicate the finite-singular-event delay floor, complete entrance census assumptions, transverse-fold integrated acceleration including both incident branches, and endpoint velocity estimate from an independently known cone, integrable full remainder and bounded projected velocity. Separate assumptions from EOM-derived facts and identify whether this advances the existing two-particle control or only a conditional class. Exclusive output analysis/mec-008-self-complement-independent-adjudication.md. Preserve accepted references, no new history or constitutive law.

2. **Coupled population evolution and preservation.** Combine independently accepted compatible summation and derivative estimates; current obstruction reviews 1–2 must first permit this route into a self-map and local existence/uniqueness argument for the entire population. Prove that environmental histories as well as targets preserve the density, moments, cancellation, source regularity, and root assumptions over one common interval. Target-only acceleration convergence is insufficient. Identify any norm in which a stability constant is uniform in population size. If EOM evolution immediately destroys the moments or loses a root margin, retain that obstruction rather than imposing a corrective background. Durable analysis: analysis/population-eom-limit.md.



3. **Admissible finite exhaustion and trajectory-error bound.** Define expanding finite populations with their complete pasts and all earlier-emission exclusions or analytic tails. Preserve the common target input, account for truncation-induced changes in environmental evolution, and prove a coupled path-error bound on common compact pre-boundary intervals. A fixed finite-memory horizon from a spiral or compact torus cannot stand in for an unbounded Euclidean population's complete past. Specify population, time-step, history-depth, and precision refinement separately. The acceptance object is a quantitative error bound or the exact obstruction that prevents one. Exclusive durable output: analysis/population-finite-exhaustion.md; effort 4's population-eom-limit.md is read-only input.



4. **Dense-class coincidence reachability or invariant exclusion.** Pursue both verdicts under the actual admitted class. Reachability requires EOM-preserved reflection-plus-polarity symmetry or another rigorous mechanism, uniform root provenance, and a finite one-sided contact limit; do not assume post-contact regular evolution to prove first contact. Exclusion requires a class-wide invariant region derived from the existing update, including an actual bound on the population remainder and any positive pair coefficient used. The shrinking transverse-velocity cone belongs only to the stated positive-coefficient subclass; polarity alone supplies no universal sign. A failure of the sum is an admissibility obstruction for that construction, never a collision-exclusion theorem. Worker develops the limiting geometry in analysis/population-coincidence-limit.md; coordinator integrates the finite/infinite distinction into the main owner.



5. **Deterministic perturbation and genericity.** Fix the admissible history topology and perturbation coordinates; prove differentiability and the rank condition of the limiting relative-position map. Distinguish a constrained symmetric slice from a neighborhood of independently perturbed histories. A rank-three map after allowing contact time can support the stated local codimension-two result; it establishes no universe-wide incidence or probability. If the rank condition or neighborhood fails at the boundary, return that precise limitation. Durable analysis: analysis/population-perturbation-transversality.md.



6. **Expanding-population EOM control.** Begin only after 5 supplies a quantitative truncation bound. Use $c_f=1$ and the shared project venv for any Python; build and monitor according to the long-job procedure. Measure acceleration-tail and derivative-tail differences, all ordered root counts/provenance, minimum range and transmitter margins, target separation and transverse velocity, areal rate, cancellation-cone status where applicable, and path differences on the common interval. Preserve rejected steps and topology changes. Freeze the analytic reference before independently authored instrumentation; same-change agreement is implementation consistency only. A failed run, sampled minimum, or absent final output is not a limiting theorem. Runtime evidence belongs in the existing ignored evidence storage. Exclusive durable interpretation: analysis/population-eom-control.md; analytical references from the reviewed summation/derivative results and efforts 4–7 remain read-only.



7. **Lineage-gated event-map continuation.** The [quintic candidate](analysis/diagonal-birth-lineage-causal-wake-candidate.md) controls certified diagonal-born same-transmitter roots on its recorded finite-order prescribed charts. Positive continuation acceptance consumes independently established smooth EOM lineage beyond the affine control accepted in the completed affine review. Test persistent tangencies, full-history sensitivities, regulator domination, event ordering, and existence/uniqueness on an open neighborhood of a declared class. This effort is independent of infinite-population admissibility and must not delay the actual self-channel reachability investigation. Exclusive durable output: analysis/mec-008-full-turn-control.md, with scripts assigned separately from effort 8 and the oracle kept fixed.



10. **Downstream consumption.** Defer new spiral reconstruction, pressure-response coefficients, Lorentz/GR residual population, and mass extraction until their existing branch/account prerequisites are met. Reuse root geometry at its honest scope. A small return residual and a spectral estimate do not prove an exact orbit; a validated EOM existence argument precedes stability. The characteristic-tail identity supplies no accepted action or Noether accounts.





### Coordinator Ownership And Dispatch

The coordinator alone edits this queue, priorities.md, brainstorming.md, work-log.md, independent-causal-wake-state-closure.md, and the shared coincide-or-not.md synthesis during the multi-agent session. Workers write complete derivations and findings to their assigned analytical owners, with assumptions, claim grades, falsifiers, and source/evidence references; they return proposed integration text for shared documents. These focused outputs are subordinate to the existing subject owners, not new competing queues. Do not create empty output files before an effort starts.

Before each dispatch, record the task name, exclusive writable paths, frozen input versions, prerequisites, and expected result in the work log. Use the existing local checkout; no linked worktree is authorized. A worker needing another worker's file sends a proposed change to the coordinator. Freeze shared definitions before dependent workers start; changes to those definitions require a coordinated revision and a new review of affected conclusions.

Dispatch current efforts 1 and 2 independently at GPT-6 Astra xhigh, with disjoint output owners and frozen mathematical subjects. The completed allocation adjudication is integrated; actual account construction remains unresolved. Other efforts remain unassigned. Substantive new theorem work and decisive reviews use explicit xhigh under the operator-approved recommendation; higher effort does not replace independent evidence.

At each integration boundary, record the full mathematical result in its owner, update the current synthesis, and move completed effort records out of the live queue into the work log, renumbering the remaining sequence and repairing dependency references. Preserve failed attempts and evidence identities. Run focused checks on the exact edited scope; generated drift caused by authored changes is left for the authorized regeneration/publication procedure. No commit, push, PR, new boundary ontology, or corpus promotion is implied by dispatch.

### MEC-008 — Same-transmitter coincidence domain reachability

- **Status:** In progress

That control refutes universal regularity over any history class that admits it, but it does not prove that a populated EOM evolution realizes the extension. Conversely, the intuition that other Architrinos continually bend the path does not yet prove exclusion: near-diagonal self-root birth is controlled first by the local chord-speed condition $\|\mathbf X_i(T)-\mathbf X_i(T-\tau)\|/\tau=c_f$ as $\tau\to0^+$, while curvature and the complete acceleration ledger enter the higher-order crossing structure. The missing object is a population-stable invariant-domain or reachability theorem on the actual retained-history phase space, with explicit quantifiers over population, initial histories, regular-root margins, and finite evolution interval. A declaration that the Master Equation simply stops before the boundary is classified as an incomplete-domain convention until that theorem or a separately accepted terminal-event ontology is supplied.
- **Required controls:** (1) the existing isolated collinear birth as a singular analytical control; (2) a minimally noncollinear three-or-more-Architrino EOM-evolved control with complete root census; (3) perturbations of population, geometry, and retained prehistory that test whether the boundary is avoided robustly or only by fine tuning; and (4) a local Taylor/chord expansion separating the necessary $\|\mathbf V_i\|=c_f$ contact from curvature-dependent higher-order terms. Every numerical instantiation uses $c_f=1$.
- **Progress, 2026-09-03; coefficient corrected 2026-09-04:** The analytical owner resolves the local secular-sign decider. On the affine pair-center, regular first-root, positive-$D_t$ chart, the complete two-row opposite-polarity contribution is $\dot{\mathbf h}_{ij}=(\kappa\lvert q_iq_j\rvert/(r^2D_t))\mathbf h=(\kappa\lvert q_iq_j\rvert c_f^2D_t/(\chi^2d^2))\mathbf h$, where $\chi=(c_f^2-\lVert\mathbf w_\perp\rVert^2/4)^{1/2}$. The positive factor $c_f^2/\chi^2=\sec^2\alpha$ corrects an earlier transverse-range omission without changing the sign: the row supplies secular gain in $\lvert h\rvert$, not drain, and $h=0$ is locally repelling. One slowly varying near-field turn contributes approximately $\kappa\lvert q_iq_j\rvert\oint(c_f^2D_t/\chi^2)\,d\theta$, reducing to $2\pi\kappa\lvert q_iq_j\rvert$ at lowest slow, near-circular symmetric order. A separate blow-up check shows that every bounded-velocity power-law time scaling capable of regularizing the inverse-square acceleration freezes the exceptional sphere and sends the root's rescaled memory span to infinity; the nondegenerate inverse-square velocity scaling exits the finite-speed chart. A universal reparameterization proof shows that, with fixed $c_f$, only absolute-time translations preserve the canonical root equation over the declared history class, and a translation cannot regularize coincidence. The unchanged EOM solver then evolved a minimally noncollinear three-architrino control through a certified $0.135183$-turn arc with 200 accepted fixed steps and 1,800 complete ordered root-accounting rows; the opposite-polarity pair's initial-normal areal-rate component rose from $0.04$ to $0.0465138089981$. This is bounded measured agreement with the sign, not the required turn average. Earlier fixed-step full-turn attempts returned no final record within their bounded process windows. Two later adaptive attempts returned fail-closed records at $0.366036$ and $0.294148$ turns because the next candidate step exhausted the root-completeness precision budget at an evolving published-history joint. Their accepted prefixes also increased the initial-normal $h$ component, but the failed responses cannot establish a turn average. Control (2), the populated invariant-region theorem, and both MEC-008 acceptance routes remain open.
- **Progress, 2026-09-03, populated-barrier continuation; velocity-domain wording corrected 2026-09-04:** A conditional invariant-region theorem now quantifies the population argument. If the exact areal-rate equation admits $\dot{\mathbf h}=\lambda\mathbf h+\boldsymbol\eta$ with $\lambda\ge L/d^2$, $\lVert\boldsymbol\eta\rVert\le Bd$, and $\lVert\mathbf w\rVert\le V$, then for sufficiently small $d$ the region $\lVert\mathbf h\rVert^2\ge\beta d$ is forward invariant and enforces $d\ge\beta/V^2$. On a monotone approach with $B>0$, any state with $\lVert\mathbf h\rVert\ge(2B/L)d^3$ is captured by such a protected region before coincidence; when $B=0$, the same holds from any nonzero $\mathbf h$. A still-unexcluded populated pair history must therefore maintain $\lVert\mathbf w_\perp\rVert=O(d^2)$ through deterministic cancellation. This is a derived conditional exclusion theorem, not yet a global one: the exact retained-history row still owes uniform $L$, $B$, and $V$ bounds and exclusion of that shrinking cone. Required control (4) is complete at local order only under an additionally assumed one-sided $\lVert\mathbf V\rVert\le c_f$ history bound. For a $C^4$ path in that class, the self-root residual has a linear deficit below field speed, a quadratic deficit while arriving at field-speed equality from below, and a cubic deficit $-\lVert\mathbf A\rVert^2\tau^3/(24c_f)+O(\tau^4)$ at nonzero equality-speed curvature. Only the straight exact-field-speed chord remains in that bounded class. The canonical Architrino velocity domain is open, so this control is not a global ceiling premise. Control (2), control (3) over arbitrary populated histories, and both MEC-008 acceptance routes remain open.
- **Progress, 2026-09-04, general-sign correction and radial first exit:** The positive secular sign is now confined to the symmetric pair-center chart. An exact locally affine arbitrary-velocity calculation yields a sign-indefinite absolute-frame center-motion term; a strictly sub-field periodic prescribed family has $\Delta h_{ij}^{\mathrm{turn}}=2\pi\kappa\lvert q_iq_j\rvert(1-2ap/k+O(\varepsilon))<0$ for $0<k<2ap$. This disproves a universal polarity-only gain theorem but does not establish EOM reachability. A separate exact-root radial theorem avoids the transverse sign: if both newest partner-root transmitter intervals have one uniform speed bound $\nu c_f$ with $\nu<1$, all remaining rows are bounded, and relative speed is bounded, then the pair's inward radial acceleration is at least $C_\nu/d^2$, where $C_\nu=2\kappa\lvert q_iq_j\rvert c_f(1-\nu)^2\sqrt{1-\nu^2}/(1+\nu)$. The resulting radial-speed inequality enforces an explicit positive separation floor before that chart can continue. Thus coincidence is excluded on the uniformly sub-field regular populated class, including its shrinking exact-aim cone. MEC-008 remains open only on the complement: field-speed or super-field newest-root histories, root-margin failure, simultaneous singular rows, loss of finite relative speed, or a certified boundary-reaching EOM history. - **Progress, 2026-09-04, causal-shadow classification:** Exact factorization of the locally affine partner-root quadratic classifies the complement of the sub-field theorem. Consequently, a symmetric inbound pair with $\mathbf v_i=-U\hat{\mathbf n}$ and $\mathbf v_j=+U\hat{\mathbf n}$, $U>c_f$, reaches prescribed affine coincidence while both ordered recent partner-root sets are empty. A further chord-residual theorem proves that a sufficiently deep bounded super-field history must nevertheless have a positive-delay self root; a constant-speed circle supplies an explicit regular example, and a finite simple-root chart with uniform margins admits a unique short DDE extension. - **Progress, 2026-09-04, exact populated reachability:** A bounded noncollinear four-Architrino retained history now realizes the causal-shadow germ. The target histories are stationary at $x=\pm2$ through $T=-1$, then join $C^1$ to $x=\pm0.01$ with inward speed $2$ at $T=0$; an opposite-polarity environmental pair is stationary at $(\pm1,1.5,0)$. ## 2026-10-05 — Curved-path wake-speed obstructions, a corrected release picture, and operator-facing capture

At the operator's instruction the same Claude session first made sure its findings are in the operator-facing pages of this directory and then continued its listed actions for a bounded period.

**Capture.** The parent README now has a section on where the unchanged equation stands across geometries, with pointers to the registry, the ledger and the synthesis. The parent queue has two new unscored rows, and the discussion record has a section on rigid balances and where a disturbed balance goes. The Braid, collinear and photon overviews point to the results. The ledger records a new operator decision, D-OP-8.

**Theorem.** The [curved-path obstruction](analysis/curved-path-wake-speed-obstructions.md) proves that a member of any arrangement that reaches wake speed at a positive rate from an entirely sub-wake past, with bounded and continuous rows from the others, has no continuation with continuous velocity, and that a member slowing to wake speed cannot continue below it. A delegated analyst wrote the proof from a stated strategy; a second delegated reviewer, who wrote a reference derivation before reading it and tested the lemmas on prescribed paths, [accepted both theorems](analysis/curved-path-wake-speed-obstructions-independent-adjudication-2026-10-05.md) with eight corrections to wording and to two subleading coefficients, all applied. Registry row COL-9, finding F-COL-9 and completed route R58 record it.

**Correction.** The first version of the [release analysis](braid-program/analysis/released-balances-and-nonrigid-search-2026-10-05.md), and the entries above that restate it, said that some released balances end in a close approach. An adaptive-step integrator shows those are fast passages, not collisions, and that the run then ends with a slower member driven to wake speed. The analysis, the registry, the ledger, the manuscripts and the trackers now carry the corrected statement. Earlier log entries are left as written.

**Releases.** A census released 39 balances. All eleven below wake speed ended, in 46 runs, with a member arriving at wake speed at a positive rate with regular roots and no change in the root census; the hypotheses of the theorem were checked on one run of each kind. The 28 with a member above wake speed gave mixed endings and pass through root births the instrument does not resolve.

**Other actions.** The photon owner has a written [assessment](photon-research/analysis/travelling-balances-assessment-2026-10-05.md) of the travelling balances, including a check of its axial identity on them, and a search found two rigid travelling arrangements of twelve members. The search for motions that are not rigid was extended to members without symmetry and found none.

**Controls and independence.** New instruments passed their controls before use: the adaptive integrator reproduces the fixed-step event time on a run with no root change, and the symmetry-free search reproduces the rigid six-member balance and the mirror class. All delegated work was produced within this session. Longer runs used the owned-compute supervisor and the owner task was closed out. A Weber campaign and a Codex session were active in shared trackers; edits here were additive. No equation was changed, no rank, score, selection or task status was altered, and nothing was staged or committed.

## 2026-10-06 — Overnight continuation of the rigid-balance thread: audit, resolved releases, a root-birth law, a second campaign

A separate Claude session continued overnight at the operator's instruction, under the unchanged Master Equation with $K=c_f=1$. Nothing was staged or committed, no rank, score, selection or task status was changed, and the field-speed ceiling variant was not run (decision D-OP-8 remains with the operator).

**Corrections first.** An adversarial audit of this session's own documents, by a delegated reviewer, returned 31 findings, three of them serious. (1) The statement that all 93 balances of the first search had been released was not supported for one three-member rotor, on which both census runs had failed; resolved runs now show that rotor disperses, so the wording that every released balance ends at wake speed is withdrawn. (2) The downward ending of the closed-form pair was overstated and is now conditioned on bounded opposing rows. (3) Identifiers this session had added collided with rows other sessions added in the same hours. They were renumbered, and entries above this one cite the old numbers: registry COL-9 is now COL-28, BIN-14 is BIN-54 and BRD-30 is BRD-34; ledger F-COL-9 and F-COL-10 are F-COL-31 and F-COL-32, F-BIN-13 and F-BIN-14 are F-BIN-69 and F-BIN-70, and F-BRD-52 to F-BRD-56 are F-BRD-58 to F-BRD-62. Two further corrections came from this night's own work: the inward ending of the closed-form pair does meet the curved-path theorem's hypotheses on the measured premises of a resolved run, contrary to the earlier statement that it lay outside them; and a candidate for a motion that is not rigid, left as a placeholder in the [release analysis](braid-program/analysis/released-balances-and-nonrigid-search-2026-10-05.md#a-search-for-periodic-motions-that-are-not-rigid), was followed and is the known planar six-member balance with a slow deformation the solver cannot remove.

**Resolved releases.** A fourth release integrator lands on every birth and merger of causal roots and constructs the new roots directly. A [delegated blind reproduction](binary-research/analysis/released-pair-blind-reproduction-2026-10-06.md) of the closed-form pair, with its own integrator, agrees with it on the arrival time to six digits ($1.844229$ periods) and on the birth time to seven. All 28 balances with a member above wake speed were released again: 55 of 56 runs completed and 38 converged between two settings, 31 with an arrival at wake speed from below, 5 with a descent to it from above, and 2 with dispersal. The 17 that did not converge pass through many root changes. The sub-wake census was repeated with the rate and transmitter factors recorded in every run: all 130 runs arrive at a positive rate with no transmitter factor below $0.21$.

**A root-birth law.** While checking the blind reproduction, the interval between a root birth and the arrival at wake speed that follows it turned out to obey a closed formula. The [note](analysis/root-birth-impulse-law.md) derives it at leading order: a local two-variable system with an exact first integral, and the time $t_*=\tau_b^4\kappa(y_*-y_0)^2(y_*+2y_0)/24$. It matches the blind measurement, made before the formula existed, to $0.1$ percent, and eight mutual releases to between one and two percent at small delay. A delegated [adjudication](analysis/root-birth-impulse-law-independent-adjudication-2026-10-06.md) re-derived the law blind, confirmed every formula, tested it on prescribed source paths for both polarity products, and required nine corrections, all applied. The most important: the first version claimed validity at every source speed, and a test at $V=100$ refuted that; the condition is that the passing distance times the passing speed be small in natural units. Ledger F-COL-33 carries it.

**A second campaign.** One hundred and one further families and 125,747 starts gave 72 more rigid balances, none free of growing roots: 165 distinct balances in all. The 61 below wake speed are enclosed. New kinds are a three-member balance at rest below wake speed with six growing roots, four four-member balances with no net polarity, a seven-member planar balance with no symmetry, and a balance travelling at $0.81$ of wake speed. Released, 120 of their 122 runs end with an arrival at wake speed and 2 disperse with every member still below wake speed, confirmed at two settings of the resolving integrator. One dispersal, of a seven-member planar balance, was followed to 30 periods: seven single architrinos, at least 77 sizes apart, every pair separating, all below wake speed, with no root born or lost. It is the first disturbed balance on record whose history does not meet the wake-speed boundary, and it is a dissolution and not an assembly (ledger F-BRD-65). In sixteen kicks each, the two dispersing balances disperse in 2 and 3. A delegated [blind reproduction](braid-program/analysis/dispersal-blind-reproduction-2026-10-06.md) with its own integrator, not told the outcomes, found the same two endings for the seven-member balance, the arrival at $0.476761$ periods and the dispersal at $2.67163$ periods. A delegated [blind check](braid-program/analysis/second-campaign-small-balances-blind-check-2026-10-06.md) certified the three-member balance and one four-member neutral balance with its own interval test and found the same counts of growing roots, six and nine, without being told them. The [search analysis](braid-program/analysis/rigid-balance-search-2026-10-04.md#a-second-campaign-2026-10-06), registry row BRD-35 and ledger F-BRD-63 carry it.

**The ring reference T02.** This session's own row sum reproduces the ring research's T02 values to ten digits. A symmetric disturbance grows at $10.60$ and $10.67$ per unit time against the certified root $10.658424$, and with three random kicks a member slows to wake speed from above after $0.83$ to $0.85$ of a period. This is a float indication for ledger route R17 and is recorded as F-BRD-64; it is not a certified trajectory.

**Where it is recorded.** The [release analysis](braid-program/analysis/released-balances-and-nonrigid-search-2026-10-05.md), the [search analysis](braid-program/analysis/rigid-balance-search-2026-10-04.md), the [root-birth law](analysis/root-birth-impulse-law.md) with its adjudication, the [closed-form pair](binary-research/analysis/asymmetric-opposite-pair-closed-form.md), the [photon assessment](photon-research/analysis/travelling-balances-assessment-2026-10-05.md), the [registry](configurations/geometry-configuration-registry.md) and [ledger](configurations/findings-ledger.md), and dated update paragraphs in the parent and Braid priorities pages, manuscripts, the synthesis and the brainstorming page. Instruments and outputs are under `.local-data/master-equation-closure/geometry-session-20261006/`. All supervised compute ran through the owned-compute supervisor under one owner task, which was closed out.

**Limits.** Every release is a float trajectory; the law is a leading-order result with no bounded remainder; the dispersed histories were followed only to 25 or 40 sizes; the delegated reviewers and this session are instances of one model family, so their agreement is not independent evidence in the strong sense.

## 2026-10-06 — Four operator-approved actions: the ceiling on released balances, a bound-history scan, the birth impulse in the integrator, and the straight-source correction

**1. The released balances under the field-speed ceiling (an equation variant, not the Master Equation).** The session read the operator's reply as approval of ledger decision D-OP-8, which the first listed action named, and recorded that reading and the frozen specification in the [variant index](equation-variants/README.md): the existing definition with the ceiling at wake speed, ordinary partner rows, zero self acceleration and the forward part removed after summation, with no event or contact rule. None stays bound. Recorded in the [release analysis](braid-program/analysis/released-balances-and-nonrigid-search-2026-10-05.md#balances-entirely-below-wake-speed).

**3. The birth impulse in the integrator (unchanged equation).** `release5.py` adds, in closed form, the velocity change the two newborn rows deliver in the step that contains a birth. The interval from a birth to the following arrival then agrees with the root-birth law to $0.1$ to $0.2$ percent, and with the blind measurement on the closed-form pair ($2.905\times10^-6$ against $2.90438\times10^-6$). Arrival times do not change at the quoted precision. Of eight previously unconverged runs repeated, two now converge, two agree but each keeps one missed root change, and the rest remain unresolved; the ring T04 was not rerun.

**4. The straight-source correction and threshold (unchanged equation).** For a straight source the two rows are known in closed form, so a free receiver obeys an ordinary differential equation. The [root-birth law](analysis/root-birth-impulse-law.md#a-straight-source-exact-rows-the-first-correction-and-the-threshold) gains: the fast-source limit as one equation in the single parameter $2/(Vb)$; a derived first correction at every source speed, $t=t_*[1+b^2V^2C]$ with $C=269/896-291/(2240(V^2-1))$ for opposite polarities and $223/2240-3/(140(V^2-1))$ for like, which agrees to five digits with the exact equation and with the adjudicator's earlier measurements; and computed thresholds, $bV$ below $1.309$ for opposite polarities and $3.330$ for like in the fast-source limit. This is a first correction and not a bound on the remainder. A second delegated [adjudication](analysis/root-birth-straight-source-independent-adjudication-2026-10-06.md) re-derived the section blind and accepted it with corrections, all applied; it corrected the opposite-polarity threshold in its fourth digit.

**Limits.** All runs are float trajectories; the ceiling test is one variation under one frozen specification; the delegated checker is an instance of the same model family. Instruments are under `.local-data/master-equation-closure/geometry-session-20261006/` in `ceiling/` and `threshold/`. Supervised compute ran under one owner task, closed out at the end.

## 2026-10-07 — Weber great-circle closure and multi-curve collocation

At about 2026-10-06T21:54Z the operator replied "do 1 2 and 3" in the session that had run the six-member investigation, whose numbered next actions were the integration, an analytical closure of the great-circle class, a multi-curve collocation at $R=1$, and a decision on the companion tasks. The investigating session read the later reply as an explicit selection of its items 2 and 3 only, bounded to three hours, and recorded that reading in the [preregistration, Section 11.1](braid-program/analysis/weber-binding-sphere-preregistration.md) and the [authorization index](equation-variants/README.md); if the operator did not intend it, the continuation is unapproved evidence.

**Integration verified.** The first run's propagation list, applied earlier by another session, was read destination by destination against its sources and found faithful; the one omission, the great-circle closest-approach theorem, is superseded by the result below.

**Results.** The [continuation synthesis](braid-program/analysis/weber-binding-sphere-continuation-2026-10-06.md) owns them. The [great-circle closure](braid-program/analysis/weber-binding-sphere-great-circle-closure.md) proves that a collision-free great-circle history of at most nine members at a common rate that satisfies the law is one planar ring in inverse-square balance, by continuing each member's equation around the complex collision times of its pairs; an [independent review](braid-program/analysis/weber-binding-sphere-great-circle-closure-review.md) re-derived every lemma, found no defect and failed to falsify it in 700 starts. The same document extends the method to uniform circular motions on the sphere at one common speed, which must be rigid rotations (independently confirmed for equal radii and for parallel axes; two independent readings and a bounded falsifier negative for unequal radii about non-parallel axes), and proves that a finite-harmonic curve of constant norm and speed is a circle. The reviewer's falsifier search over unequal radii about non-parallel axes (333 starts, reach cases recovered) found no non-rigid state below residual $0.23$. The [multi-curve collocation](braid-program/analysis/weber-binding-sphere-multicurve-collocation.md), [adjudicated](braid-program/analysis/weber-binding-sphere-multicurve-adjudication.md) with the frozen reference library, passes its known cases including two non-rigid closed rosettes. The multi-curve collocation at $R=1$ found no candidate other than the hexagon in 625 target starts at three harmonics and no residual falling with the number of harmonics on 47 laddered states, a measured weak negative. A defect of the preregistered residual normalization, found by the collocation lane, was the Principal Investigator's specification error and is amended in the preregistration, Section 11.6.

**Carried.** Equation-variants manuscript Section 9 and its taxonomy and decisions rows; Braid Program manuscript Section 12.2; registry rows BRD-W-I-4 and BRD-W-I-5; ledger rows F-W-I-10 to F-W-I-12; the parent and Braid Program priorities; the Braid Program queue's stopped-campaign entry; the brainstorming disposition. No canon adoption, rank change, generated rewrite or Git publication was made.

## 2026-10-09 — Weber follow-ups: P-W-2 agreed with qualifications; P-W-3 evaluated on retained trajectories

## 2026-10-09 — Weber follow-up P-W-3 closed: sampled results agreed, dense continuations not converged

Codex reproduced the sampled-state results of the [window analysis](braid-program/analysis/weber-pair-external-acceleration-window.md) with a separately authored diagnostic and accepted the leading acceleration-coupling term with qualifications, which were applied. Two statements in the preceding entry are corrected here and left as written there: the distance of more than 128 from every other member holds at the recorded states, not continuously; and finding F-W-I-16 is no longer "proposed" but stands with a derived leading term for bounded velocities and accelerations and sampled diagnostics measured at recorded states. Codex then ran the two prepared dense continuations of the recorded window-start state under the operator's authorization, at step sizes 0.002 and 0.001. They failed the convergence test Codex fixed before launch: the carried signed integrals are -0.297 against -0.186 and +0.300 against +0.189. Each run satisfies the identity internally and passes the bound-pair diagnostic at every step, but the two disagree, so there is no converged integral over the window and no certificate of persistence between recorded states. This is recorded as non-convergence of the integrator at those steps, not as chaos or instability. The prepared launch lacked a convergence ladder, shared-time checkpoints and retained end states; a revised numerical plan is recorded as a remaining burden and was not undertaken. The inquiry is complete at these boundaries by agreement with Codex.

## 2026-10-09 — Weber follow-up P-W-4: root-existence hypotheses audited

The fourth inquiry was carried out as a bounded read-only [audit](analysis/root-census-hypothesis-audit-2026-10-09.md) of how documents under this priority justify the existence of a partner root. A [screen](evidence/root-census-hypothesis-screen.mjs) over 1727 Markdown files, run after its four known cases passed (the first attempt failed one case, and the offending pattern was removed), found 1081 paragraphs in 563 files asserting a root and flagged 90 whose speed wording is pointwise only. The audit separates what pointwise strict speed gives (at most one root, no own-path root) from what existence needs, lists four sufficient conditions, and records that the second reading of the mirror-pair angular momentum proof had found and corrected the same defect on 2026-10-05 without the correction reaching the Weber pair investigation.
